# 1. Executive finding & architectural determination

### Does the Master Comic Generation Prompt remain necessary as a dedicated specification?
**Yes.** The Master Comic Generation Prompt must remain a specialized, standalone derivative-media specification within the Classies Chronicles technical production ecosystem. It must not be collapsed into the Daily Chronicle Generation Protocol (`CC-DCGP v0.08.00`) or treated as an automatic stage of daily text generation.

The architectural separation of concerns is clear:
1. `CC-UPMP v0.08.00` is the **creative, canonical, and governance authority**. It establishes the world, locked canon, three-clock model, information classification (Output A/B/C), high-level derivative boundaries, and specialist acceptance gates (`C01–C22`).
2. `CC-DCGP v0.08.00` is the **daily execution runbook**. It governs deterministic 10-phase narrative drafting, source evidence snapshots, daily validation gates (`Q01–Q16`), and text handover.
3. `CC-ECLPM v0.08.00` is the **episode-owned continuity and ledger authority**. It maintains immutable state across 19 logical record families, controls the portable B6 delta, tracks downstream derivative staleness, and quarantines restricted knowledge.
4. `CC-MCGP v0.08.00` is the **specialized sequential-art adaptation engine**. It governs page allocation, cinematic visual pacing, mobile-first 9:16 geometry, sizeable typography, 4x4 master collage rendering, precision slicing, terminal challenge placement, and pixel-level gate verification.

### Core Upgrades Delivered in v0.08.00
- **Alignment with v0.08.00 Suite:** Full synchronization with the v0.08.00 technical production set, resolving all historical v0.03.01 and v0.04.00 naming discrepancies, gate collisions, and ledger mappings.
- **The 16-Page Mobile Story Standard (Note 2):** Complete transition from desktop-centric ~5-page comics to a **16-page mobile story standard** ($W \times H = 9:16$) tailored for vertical mobile viewing (WhatsApp Statuses, Facebook Stories, Snapchat, Instagram Stories). Establishes the fundamental equivalence: **4 mobile pages = 1 previous standard comic page**.
- **The 4x4 Master Collage & Slicing Architecture:** Formalization of the single-image 4x4 master collage workflow. Demonstrates the mathematical proof that a $4 \times 4$ grid of 9:16 portrait pages preserves an **exact 9:16 overall aspect ratio**, enabling one-pass high-resolution rendering and precision slicing.
- **Strict Implementation of C01–C22 Specialist Gates:** Articulates all 22 specialist acceptance gates established in CC-UPMP v0.08.00 Section 31, mapping results directly into Output B8 and the ledger `validation_report`.
- **Absolute Terminal Challenge Invariant:** Re-enforces that the public challenge appears once, at the absolute bottom of Page 16 (or final page), with literally zero text or graphics below it and zero answer leakage.

---

# 4. Material conflict register and disposition

### Conflict 1: Automatic Comic Generation vs. Explicit Commissioning
- **Historical Tension:** Earlier proposals suggested that completing a daily chronicle should immediately launch comic storyboard and image generation.
- **Resolution in v0.08.00:** Strictly rejected. `CC-UPMP v0.08.00` Section 28 and `CC-DCGP v0.08.00` Section 2 mandate that daily chronicle generation must **never** automatically trigger comic rendering. Sequential art is resource-intensive and requires explicit commissioning, an identified source revision, and an approved page count. Mode `COMIC_REVIEW` or `COMIC_PLAN` does not authorize `COMIC_GENERATE`.

### Conflict 2: Desktop 5-Page Standard vs. Mobile Story Readability (Note 2)
- **Historical Tension:** v0.03.01 and v0.04.00 utilized a default 5-page standard with 3–5 panels per page. On mobile displays (WhatsApp Status, Facebook Stories, Snapchat), crowding 4 panels with dialogue balloons onto a vertical screen resulted in microscopic, unreadable text.
- **Resolution in v0.08.00:** Fully resolved by adopting **Note 2: The 16-Page Mobile Story Standard**. One standard chronicle maps to **16 mobile comic pages** ($W \times H = 9:16$). Exactly **4 mobile pages correspond to 1 previous standard comic page**. This allows each mobile page to feature 1–3 large panels with sizeable typography ($\ge 28$pt) and clear mobile safe areas.

### Conflict 3: Multi-Panel Grid Mathematics vs. Portrait Geometry
- **Historical Tension:** Older drafts ambiguously referred to "vertical 16:9" pages and suggested grids without analyzing aspect ratio distortion.
- **Resolution in v0.08.00:** Corrected in CC-UPMP Section 30 and CC-MCGP Section 22. Every individual page is **9:16 portrait** ($H = \frac{16}{9}W$). A uniform grid of $C$ columns and $R$ rows has aspect ratio $\frac{9C}{16R}$. When $C=4, R=4$, the collage aspect ratio is $\frac{36}{64} = \mathbf{9:16}$. Thus, a 4x4 master collage shares the exact aspect ratio of an individual page, fitting vertical mobile screens perfectly during review and slicing cleanly into 16 individual 9:16 mobile story pages.

### Conflict 4: Collision between Daily Gates (Q) and Comic Gates (C)
- **Historical Tension:** Some AI platform responses attempted to renumber or merge daily chronicle gates (`Q01–Q16`) and comic gates (`C01–C22`), creating namespace collisions.
- **Resolution in v0.08.00:** Strictly maintained as separate namespaces. `Q01_SCOPE` through `Q16_BOUNDARIES` govern the daily text chronicle. `C01_COMMISSION` through `C22_DISCLOSURE` govern comic visual adaptation. Comic QA results are logged in Output B8 and the ledger `validation_report` with the `C` namespace explicitly preserved.

### Conflict 5: Terminal Challenge Placement vs. Mobile Platform UI
- **Historical Tension:** Previous drafts permitted occasional footer notes, page counters, or promotional links below the final challenge.
- **Resolution in v0.08.00:** Inviolable rule enforced: The verbatim public challenge appears **once, at the absolute bottom of Page 16 (or final page), with literally zero visible text, branding, or graphics printed below it**. Safe margin rules reserve the bottom 25%–30% of Page 16 so that native mobile status reply bars do not occlude the quiz text.

---

# 5. Detailed platform-response synthesis and adjudication

We synthesize the contributions from across platforms in Compilation 1, 2, 3 (`CC-Master_Comic_Generation_Prompt_development_responses-3.txt`), and 4:

### 5.1 GitHub Submissions (`GITHUB-1` & `GITHUB-2`)
- **Key Contributions Adopted:**
  - Absolute enforcement of the governance boundary: the comic prompt is a derivative expression, not an independent story creator.
  - Strict mathematical formulation of the 9:16 aspect ratio ($H/W = 16/9$).
  - Ban on visible scene/panel numbering inside the visible artwork.
  - Separation between master collage delivery and individual sliced file inventories.
- **Rejected Claims:** Proposals to auto-commit rendered assets to GitHub without human editorial review were rejected as violations of repository security.

### 5.2 ChatGPT Submissions (`CHATGPT-1`, `CHATGPT-2`, `CHATGPT-3`)
- **Key Contributions Adopted:**
  - Four-act, sixteen-beat narrative rhythm mapping 4 mobile pages to each dramatic phase.
  - Sizing standards for mobile typography: minimum font size equivalent to 28–34pt at 1080x1920 px resolution; maximum 45 words per page.
  - High-contrast speech balloon styling with solid opaque backgrounds and dark borders.
  - Invalidation state machine marking dependent comic assets `STALE_REVALIDATION_REQUIRED` whenever Output A text changes.
- **Refined Material:** Suggestions for an open-ended page count were bounded by clear commission classes (4, 8, 16, 20–24 pages).

### 5.3 Jules Independent Engineering Submissions (`JULES-1` & `JULES-2`)
- **Key Contributions Adopted:**
  - Mathematical proof of the $4 \times 4$ collage aspect ratio invariance ($\text{Ratio} = 9:16$).
  - Operational handling of platform API generation quotas: utilizing a single 4x4 master collage render to overcome per-session quota limits while preventing character style drift.
  - Precision slicing coordinate matrix ($P = r \times 4 + c + 1$) and gutter margin rules (2%–4% safety margin).
  - Strict enforcement of S00E01 canonical hardware limits (Edubeam energy constraints, no teleportation).
- **Corrected Material:** Early drafts suggested an 8-page standard; refined to the 16-page mobile story standard in accordance with Note 2.

### 5.4 Perplexity Submissions (`PERPLEXITY-1`)
- **Key Contributions Adopted:**

  - Strict ledger mapping to the 19 canonical record families in CC-ECLPM v0.08.00; rejection of unauthorized 20th record families.

# 7. Mobile geometry, 16-page standard, and slicing architectural decision

### 7.1 The Mobile Consumption Dilemma
Sequential art in the Classies Chronicles franchise is primarily distributed across mobile communication channels:
- WhatsApp Statuses (auto-advancing vertical story cards).
- Facebook Stories / Instagram Stories (swipeable full-screen mobile viewports).
- Mobile web readers (vertical scroll / card swipe).

When traditional 5-page desktop comic layouts were loaded onto a mobile phone screen:
1. Four to six panels per page forced speech bubbles down to 10–12pt equivalent, rendering dialogue illegible without manual pinching and zooming.
2. Emotional facial expressions and crucial prop details were lost in tiny sub-panels.
3. Rapid status consumption (3–5 seconds per screen) was disrupted by visual clutter.

### 7.2 The 16-Page Mobile Solution (Note 2)
By expanding the daily chronicle adaptation into **16 mobile comic pages**, each dramatic beat receives dedicated screen real estate:
- **Pacing:** Each mobile page acts as a discrete dramatic "card" or beat.
- **Typography:** Dialogue balloons feature large, bold lettering ($\ge 28$pt) that is immediately legible on small handheld screens.
- **Composition:** 1 to 3 cinematic panels per screen ensure clear focal points and dramatic visual hierarchy.
- **Narrative Equivalence:** 4 mobile pages cleanly encapsulate one full narrative act, establishing the robust equivalence:
  $$\mathbf{1\text{ Chronicle} = 4\text{ Acts} = 16\text{ Mobile Pages} \quad (\approx 4\text{ Traditional Comic Pages})}$$

### 7.3 The 4x4 Master Collage Synthesis
Generating 16 separate images via AI image APIs introduces severe operational friction:
- Exceeding session rate limits (many APIs restrict bursts to 2–4 images).
- Style drift and facial inconsistency between separate rendering calls.
- High operational overhead in managing 16 independent prompt dispatches.

The **4x4 Master Collage Sheet** elegantly resolves this:
1. **Mathematical Invariant:**
   $$\text{Collage Width} = 4W, \quad \text{Collage Height} = 4H = 4 \times \left(\frac{16}{9}W\right) = \frac{64}{9}W$$
   $$\text{Collage Aspect Ratio} = \frac{4W}{\frac{64}{9}W} = \frac{36}{64} = \mathbf{\frac{9}{16}}$$
   The master sheet itself possesses an **exact 9:16 portrait aspect ratio**!
2. **One-Pass Consistency:** All 16 pages are rendered simultaneously within a unified lighting, color, and character model environment.
3. **Precision Extraction:** The sheet is sliced into 16 discrete 9:16 files using automated CLI tools or Python scripts, perfectly prepared for mobile status uploads.

---

# 8. Challenge invariant, knowledge firewall, and blind QA logic

The audience challenge is safety-critical for the franchise's educational and competitive integrity:
1. **Verbatim Fidelity:** Gate `C15_CHALLENGE_SOURCE` mandates exact textual matching with Output A and `challenge_public_register`. Paraphrasing or shortening to fit layout constraints is strictly prohibited.
2. **Terminal Invariant:** Gate `C16_CHALLENGE_PLACEMENT` mandates that the challenge appears at the absolute bottom of Page 16, with literally nothing printed below it. This eliminates cognitive clutter and ensures seamless handoff to audience interactive voting.
3. **Knowledge Firewall & Blind Audit:** Gate `C17_NO_LEAKAGE` enforces a blind review protocol. Neither the adapter nor the validator accesses Output C (`challenge_answer_key`). Instead, the artwork is audited to ensure that no character gesture, color highlight, background prop, or metadata clue distinguishes any single option over the others.

---

# 9. Production manifest, dependency invalidation state machine, and handover protocol

In accordance with `CC-ECLPM v0.08.00`, comic assets are registered within the `production_manifest` logical family:
- Asset files, image hashes, pixel resolutions, and slice inventories are logged under Output B7.
- Acceptance gate evaluations (`C01` to `C22`) are recorded under Output B8.
- The invalidation state machine enforces that if Output A narrative text is amended, all dependent comic assets transition to `STALE_REVALIDATION_REQUIRED`.
- Rendering artwork creates a `RENDERED_DRAFT`; only explicit human editorial review can elevate assets to `APPROVED_FOR_ASSEMBLY` or `RELEASE_READY`.

# PART I — AUTHORIZATION, MODES AND SOURCE CONTRACT

## 1. Production role and creative mission

This prompt governs the production of **Classies Chronicles comic pages** derived from an authorized daily chronicle (Output A). It is the sole authoritative operating instruction for comic generation within the CC production suite. Its purpose is to translate one accepted daily narrative into **individual 9:16 portrait comic pages** — formatted for direct sharing on WhatsApp Status, Facebook Stories, Snapchat and equivalent mobile-first platforms — and optionally into a **single collage image** from which individual pages may be manually sliced.

The default commission for an ordinary chronicle is **16 individual 9:16 pages per chronicle**. Four 9:16 pages at adequate resolution are the mobile-first equivalent of one prior landscape/A4-style page. This replaces the prior working default of 5 pages and reflects the mobile-platform requirement established in the v0.08 production set.

This prompt does not generate chronicles, media assets, challenge keys or archive records autonomously. It is activated only by an explicit authorized commission referencing a specific Output A source. A document review or framework-update request produces documentation only — no images.

## 2. Operation classifier: never infer a commission from a document review

| Mode | Trigger | Output |
|---|---|---|
| `REVIEW` | Request to update, analyze or improve this prompt or companion documents | Revised documentation only; no images generated or claimed |
| `PLAN` | Explicit request for a comic plan, brief or outline without rendering | Beat-coverage plan, page allocation and risk report; no images |
| `STORYBOARD` | Explicit storyboard commission | Per-page specifications, lettering plan, challenge plan; no rendered images |
| `GENERATE` | Explicit commission with a named authorized Output A source | Actual rendered 9:16 pages and/or collage, per-page QA, delivery inventory |
| `REVISE` | Explicit revision of specific existing pages | Modified assets, dependency/QA evidence; old versions preserved |
| `ASSEMBLE` | Explicit collage/scroll/export commission | Ordered composite with per-cell geometry verification and inventory |
| `VALIDATE` | Explicit QA request on existing assets | Per-page C01–C22 pass/fail report |

A `GENERATE` commission requires: named authorized Output A revision, explicit page count N (or acceptance of the 16-page default), explicit or inferable collage requirement, confirmed or documented rights snapshot. A document-only request — including this update request — is `REVIEW` mode and produces no images.

## 3. Per-commission input resolution and priority

Resolve the following before any storyboard or rendering work begins:

1. **Exact Output A source** — episode ID, revision/hash, editorial status (`DRAFT`, `ACCEPTED`, `APPROVED_FOR_PUBLICATION`). Never infer approval from episode title or prior chat history.
2. **Page count N** — exact commissioned integer controls; defaults apply only when no count is stated (16 standard, 3 short, 7–8 extended, 9 complex, 10–12 exceptional, 13+ special). The count refers to **individual narrative pages**, not render calls or composite cells.
3. **Delivery form** — individual pages, collage, both, or vertical scroll. Defaults: both individual pages and a 2×8 collage, subject to renderer capability.
4. **Rights and consent snapshot** — visual canon, personal-portrayal consents, brand licenses, applicable Edubeam era constraints, challenge public register availability.
5. **B6 portable handover** from prior session if continuing a multi-session commission.

Priority: explicit user commission > B6 handover context > prior session memory > documented defaults.

## 4. Exact narrative source and allowable derivative status

| Source classification | Permitted comic use |
|---|---|
| `ACCEPTED` Output A with confirmed public challenge | Full commission, all C gates eligible for PASS |
| `DRAFT` Output A, authorized internal test run | Draft comic pages, clearly marked `DRAFT — NOT FOR PUBLICATION`; no approval claim |
| Excerpt of Output A (incomplete source) | Excerpt-only comic; disclose coverage limitations; no false full-adaptation claim |
| No Output A available | No comic generation; BLOCKED — request authorized source |
| Restricted Output C | **Never use** — challenge answer firewall is absolute |

The public challenge text is taken exclusively from the exact selected Output A public text (and reconciled against `challenge_public_register` when accessible). No paraphrase, extraction from C, or invention is permitted.

## 5. Security and rights wall

| Wall | Rule |
|---|---|
| Challenge answer key (Output C) | Never fetch, read, use, approximate or include in any output |
| Personal diary / private records | Exclude entirely without verified consent; fictional role/workplace is acceptable |
| Personal likeness and voice | Require verified consent scoped to use type, platform and date; provisional draft only otherwise |
| Third-party brand / logo | Actual usage rights only; no reproduction of protected marks |
| Private/internal B packet material | Never include in public Output A comic pages |
| Real future news / fabricated telemetry | Clearly label fictional; no authentic-seeming displays of false data |

---

# PART II — STORY AND CONTINUITY TRANSFER

## 6. Source beat map and fidelity register

Before storyboarding, extract a **beat map** from Output A: each narrative turning point, dialogue moment, environmental shift, character decision, technical demonstration and the terminal public challenge. Assign each beat to one or more planned pages. A beat map entry requires: beat description, causal link to prior beat, character(s) present, location, clock type (A/B/C), emotional register and whether it carries the public challenge.

Fidelity rules:
- Every essential beat must appear in at least one page.
- No consequential event may be invented that is absent from Output A.
- Omit a non-essential beat rather than fabricate a replacement.
- For a 16-page commission, fine-grained development of existing beats (close reactions, environmental context, dialogue staging, prop detail) legitimately fills the additional pages; do not add fabricated plot events.
- Report `FIDELITY_RISK` if the page count cannot be met without consequential filler; reduce count or use fine-grained development.

## 7. Canon and visual reference register

For each character, location, technology and object appearing in the planned pages, record:

| Field | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Reference type | `APPROVED_CANON` / `EPISODE_ESTABLISHED` / `PROVISIONAL_DRAFT` / `UNVERIFIED` |
| Source file | Actual accessible file path or URL; `NOT_RETRIEVED` if unavailable |
| Consent/rights status | Applicable consent scope, date, platform and revocation flag |
| Era lock | Applicable Edubeam era and capability constraints (Foundation Phase limitations apply) |
| Version | Canon version or episode number where appearance was established |

A `PROVISIONAL_DRAFT` reference may be used for an authorized internal draft only; it must be labeled and cannot be promoted to `APPROVED_CANON` by this prompt alone.

## 8. The three-clock visual continuity system

Every page panel must be assignable to exactly one clock and modality:

| Clock | Location | Content type | Visual label convention |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Clock A (Production)** | New Legon, Accra (Koware HQ) | Real current events, workplace scenes, James Evans daily life | Present tense; verified real-world observable facts only |
| **Clock B (Historical)** | Las Vegas, Nevada (Enka historical) | Archival research, memory, historical reflection | Archive/sepia treatment optional; clearly not current news |
| **Clock C (Probe)** | Kuala Lumpur, Malaysia (Millennium Probe destination) | Probe readings, oscillating measurement outputs | Labeled as `PROBE DATA`; fictional in-universe instrument; no fake authoritative UI |

Modalities: `PHYSICAL_PRESENT` (character actually there), `MEMORY_RECALL` (character remembers), `FICTIONAL_FUTURE` (in-universe speculative narrative), `RESEARCH_VISUALIZATION` (illustrating data/documents). Never conflate clocks or present a fictional future frame as a current real-world event.

## 9. Edubeam, mission, era and hardware continuity

- **Foundation Phase (S00) constraints apply.** Advanced capabilities (extended era travel beyond ±250 years, self-teleportation, object transfer from probe destination) are provisional story leads, not established physics in Foundation episodes. Do not depict them as confirmed operating capabilities.
- **Mission roster state.** A team member remains at their last source-documented deployment point until an explicit authorized return event. Never draw a returned traveler without an accepted return record.
- **Hardware continuity.** Device state, damage, repairs and upgrades carry across pages; do not reset without a source event.
- **Era-specific costume and environment.** Verify era for any historical or probe-destination scene against B3/B5 accepted records.

## 10. Other continuity: environments, evidence, roles and future fiction

- **Environment.** Sets, lighting, weather, geography and period-accurate props must be consistent within and across pages of the same episode unless a transition is explicitly depicted.
- **Organizational roles.** James Evans (Director of Academic Programs), Steven Sarpong (Academic Registrar), Matthew Ebenezer (Academic Partnerships & Outreach Officer) and other org chart roles are source-verified; never assign fictional responsibilities.
- **Evidence and documents.** Any on-page document, screen, report or data display must be either (a) directly sourced from Output A text, (b) clearly fictional and labeled, or (c) a generic non-attributed prop. Never fabricate authentic-looking official data, news, scripture values or probe readings.
- **Future fiction.** In-universe speculative content (e.g. projected probe arrival, mission objectives) is labeled as fictional narrative; it is not real-world prediction.

---

# PART III — PAGE ALLOCATION, STORYBOARDING AND VISUAL GRAMMAR

## 11. Page count selection and narrative coverage

**Default: 16 individual 9:16 pages for a standard chronicle.** This reflects the mobile-first production standard where 4 pages at adequate 9:16 resolution replace one prior landscape/A4 page for WhatsApp Status, Facebook Stories and Snapchat delivery.

| Input situation | Editorial allocation rule |
|---|---|
| Exact authorized count stated | Use that exact positive integer; disclose partial fulfillment if capability blockers arise |
| Short chronicle, no fixed count | **4–6 pages** |
| Standard/unspecified, no fixed count | **16 pages** (mobile-first default) |
| Extended narrative | **20–24 pages** |
| Long/complex chronicle | **24–28 pages** |
| Exceptional/multi-strand | **Up to 32 pages** with story justification |
| Special explicit commission | Any stated N; map each page to an identifiable story function |

**Page allocation within 16 pages (standard template):**

| Page(s) | Narrative function |
|---|---|
| 1 | Hook / chapter title splash — opening establishing beat |
| 2–4 | Orientation — location, character introduction, situation setup |
| 5–7 | Development / complication — advancing story tensions |
| 8–10 | Central event / decision — key dramatic action or revelation |
| 11–13 | Consequences / changed state — reactions, ripple effects |
| 14–15 | Reflection / forward tension — character processing, next stakes |
| 16 | Final challenge page — story closure beat + exact terminal public challenge |

This template is a planning scaffold, not a rigid grid. Beats may shift by ±1 page based on actual source coverage. What does not shift: page 16 (or the final page) carries the terminal public challenge and nothing follows it.

Word count alone does not determine page count. Evaluate turning points, concurrent character strands, environments, time transitions, dialogue weight, visual evidence, technical demonstrations and the challenge text.

## 12. Five-function dramatic rhythm and page-purpose test

Every page must serve one or more of: **orientation/hook**, **development/complication**, **central event/decision**, **consequences/changed state**, **reflection/forward tension + audience challenge**. Before finalizing a storyboard, answer for each page:

1. What changes for the reader?
2. What happened in Output A that legitimizes this page?
3. Why is this page separate from its neighbors?
4. What knowledge, location or object is now different?
5. What must the next page preserve?

A duplicate spectacle shot with no new insight fails page-purpose review. Report `PAGE_PURPOSE_FAIL` and replace with a distinct narrative moment.

## 13. Individual page geometry and reading order

Every finished **individual page** is a complete, independently legible **9:16 portrait** canvas:

- **Preferred resolution:** 1080 × 1920 px (other exact integer multiples of 9:16 acceptable when resolution is sufficient)
- **Verification:** `width × 16 == height × 9` (or documented export rounding tolerance)
- **Content bounds:** All text (speech, captions, challenge), panel edges, gutters and any titles must fall wholly within the canvas — never cropped at edge
- **Safe margins:** 72 px minimum on all sides for device UI occlusion, overlays and potential platform trim
- **Mobile readability:** Every meaningful text element legible at actual mobile viewing scale (minimum effective font size ~16 sp equivalent at 1080 px wide)
- **Reading order:** Left-to-right, top-to-bottom (English); for other scripts, establish explicitly

Do not stretch, warp or after-the-fact trim to force the ratio. Recompose or pad rather than crop faces or challenge text. A full-page image with a tiny inset comic is not an acceptable way to evade geometry requirements.

## 14. Cinematic panel hierarchy (A–E)

- **A. Major event** — full-page spread or dominant full-height cinematic panel for high-stakes decision, reveal, emotional climax or dramatic physical action
- **B. Significant development** — medium/large panel for important interaction, discovery or response
- **C. Supporting action** — bridging visual for location, cause, transition or process
- **D. Notable detail** — inset/close-up for eye/hand, device, evidence, clue, expression or object
- **E. Contextual information** — restrained timeline/location tag, verified interface, caption or compact schematic

**3–5 panels per page is a useful rhythm, not a compulsory grid.** Full-page splash, 2-panel confrontation, several medium panels or a sequence of smaller details are all valid based on narrative weight. Preserve left/right blocking, eyelines, continuity of light/shadow and object position across panels. Dynamic gutters and irregular panels are allowed when the eye path remains unambiguous.

## 15. Visual identity, typography and permitted marks

**Art direction:** Original cinematic graphic-novel style — deliberate contrast, expressive anatomy, atmospheric lighting, vivid yet controlled color, coherent environment design, believable action. Align with approved Classies Chronicles identity assets only; do not invent official marks from a brand name.

**Typography and lettering:**
- Attribute each bubble correctly: speech, thought, narration and verified interface text are visually distinct
- No overlap between bubbles and faces/key actions
- Readable contrast and adequate size at mobile scale
- Condense dialogue faithfully without changing intent, voice or factual content
- Sound effects may add expressive atmosphere but must not insert unexplained physical events
- No decorative machine readout that invents scientific telemetry

**Where generator text is unreliable:** Reserve blank balloon/caption spaces in artwork → overlay verified typeset dialogue/challenge using capable tool → visually inspect final raster. Garbled text, truncated bubbles or broken glyphs = `CHANGES_REQUIRED`, not publication-ready.

**Numbering and marks:**
- No printed **scene or panel numbers** in finished pages
- Internal storyboard page/beat/panel indexes are required for production management
- Optional visible `Page X of Y`, series logo or title: requires explicit current brief, verified rights and approved visual spec; suppress all visible page counters if the user excludes numbering
- **Nothing** below the terminal challenge: no copyright line, logo, byline, footer, credit, arrow, navigation, QR code, page number or social link

---

# PART IV — EXACT PUBLIC CHALLENGE AND RESTRICTED-KNOWLEDGE FIREWALL

## 16. Public challenge source-of-record reconciliation

Before storyboarding the final page:

1. Extract the **entire public audience challenge** from the exact selected Output A revision — preserving wording, punctuation, numerals, answer choices and ordering
2. Locate the corresponding `challenge_public_register` entry in the approved episode archive (when accessible)
3. Compare the two versions **exactly**

| Condition | Required disposition |
|---|---|
| Output A and register agree | `PUBLIC_CHALLENGE_RECONCILED`; proceed to final-page layout |
| Register unavailable, Output A complete | Clearly marked **provisional draft** final page; record `REGISTER_UNAVAILABLE — NOT FULLY RECONCILED`; no editorial/publication claim |
| Wording/choices/status differ | `exception_conflict_register` entry; `BLOCKED — PUBLIC CHALLENGE CONFLICT` until authorized resolution |
| No complete public challenge in Output A | Do not invent; request corrected source; non-challenge pages may continue as draft |
| Source expressly authorizes different challenge | Treat as separately approved source revision with evidence |

Never paraphrase, shorten, reorder, split across pages or translate the challenge without an authorized corrected public revision.

## 17. Final-page placement invariant

The public challenge text appears:
- **Exactly once**
- **On the last page of the comic** (page 16 in a standard commission, or page N)
- **After the story's final dramatic beat** — earned narrative closure must precede the question
- **At the bottom of the final page** as the terminal visual element
- With **nothing visible below it** — no post-challenge element of any kind

If the challenge is long (multiple sentences + multiple-choice options), allocate sufficient panel space. Never truncate the challenge. If legibility requires a reduced panel count on the final page to allow full challenge text, that is correct design.

## 18. Answer leakage review without inspecting the secret

Before finalizing each page, perform a **public-only semantic scan**:
- Read the public challenge question and answer options
- Review each panel for visual elements that directly or indirectly indicate the correct answer (object positions, character gestures, highlighted items, facial expressions on specific options, color coding)
- If a risk is identified, revise the panel; do not look up Output C
- If the risk cannot be evaluated without the answer, mark `C17: BLOCKED — REQUIRES AUTHORIZED HUMAN REVIEW` and route to the challenge reviewer through the approved secure process
- The reviewer returns only a non-revealing `PASS` / `FAIL` decision

The comic workflow never releases, accesses, approximates or derives the answer key.

---

# PART V — PRODUCTION LIFECYCLE, PAGE SPECIFICATIONS AND DELIVERY

## 19. Nine-stage execution pipeline

| Stage | Action | Completion evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **S1. Commission intake** | Verify Output A revision, N, delivery form, rights snapshot, B6 context | All inputs documented; blockers identified |
| **S2. Beat map** | Extract all beats from Output A; assign to pages | Beat-to-page coverage table complete; FIDELITY_RISK noted if any |
| **S3. Storyboard** | Per-page panel plan, lettering plan, clock/modality labels, challenge plan | Storyboard doc with page-purpose test results |
| **S4. Canon check** | Verify each character, location, tech and object reference classification | Canon register entries; provisional items marked |
| **S5. Rights check** | Verify consent, license and rights for all depicted elements | Rights snapshot; excluded elements listed |
| **S6. Challenge reconciliation** | Compare Output A challenge and public register | `PUBLIC_CHALLENGE_RECONCILED` or documented conflict |
| **S7. Rendering** | Generate actual 9:16 pages per storyboard | Actual rendered assets; partial inventory if incomplete |
| **S8. QA** | Per-page C01–C22 checks on actual pixels | QA table with PASS/FAIL/NOT_CHECKED per gate |
| **S9. Delivery** | Compile individual pages + collage; generate handover record | Delivery manifest; asset count vs commissioned count |

Stages S1–S6 and S9 may be executed without a rendering tool. S7 requires an active image generator. Report actual completion state; never claim a later stage completed when an earlier stage is blocked.

## 20. Minimum internal page specification

For each page, maintain:

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Page index | Integer 1–N |
| Beat coverage | Beat IDs from beat map covered by this page |
| Panel layout | A–E hierarchy, panel count, dominant composition |
| Characters | Names, canon/provisional status, visual reference |
| Location | Named location, clock (A/B/C), modality |
| Key visual | Primary visual element (action, object, expression) |
| Dialogue/caption plan | Abbreviated planned text per bubble/caption |
| Challenge | `N/A` or `TERMINAL_CHALLENGE` (final page only) |
| Asset ref | Actual file path or `NOT_RENDERED` |
| QA status | Per-gate C01–C22 result or `NOT_CHECKED` |
| Dependency state | `CURRENT` / `STALE_REVALIDATION_REQUIRED` / `UNVERIFIED` |

## 21. Per-page renderer directive

When generating each 9:16 page, the renderer directive must specify:

1. **Canvas:** `1080 × 1920 px, 9:16 portrait, RGB`
2. **Style:** Original cinematic graphic-novel; deliberate contrast; atmospheric lighting; detailed character expressions; [approved Classies Chronicles art direction reference when available]
3. **Page content:** Scene description, character positions, panel layout (A–E), dialogue/caption plan, clock/location context
4. **Text regions:** Blank speech bubbles and caption boxes if reliable text overlay is needed
5. **Challenge text (final page only):** Exact verbatim public challenge text; large legible font; terminal position; nothing below
6. **Constraints:** No scene/panel numbers; no elements below final challenge; safe margins maintained; no fabricated data displays

Verify actual rendered dimensions before reporting delivery. A storyboard description is not a rendered page.

## 22. Multi-page sheets, collages, long-scrolls and mobile exports

The individual page format and the assembled container are **different geometries**. The default delivery for a 16-page commission is **both** individual pages **and** a single collage image (2×8 grid of 9:16 cells).

### A. Separate-page pipeline (preferred for quality)

Render N complete 9:16 pages individually. Verify each. Assemble collage as a secondary output after individual pages are confirmed. Exact page count applies to **real pages**, not render calls.

### B. Grid/spritesheet pipeline (conditional on renderer capability)

If rendering N pages individually exceeds tool limits, a uniform M-column × R-row grid may be used:

| Commission | Grid | Outer sheet geometry | Cell size requirement |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4 pages | 2×2 | 2160 × 3840 px (9:16 outer) | 1080 × 1920 px each |
| 8 pages | 2×4 | 2160 × 7680 px (9:32 outer) | 1080 × 1920 px each |
| 16 pages | 2×8 | 2160 × 15360 px (9:64 outer) | 1080 × 1920 px each |
| 16 pages (alt) | 4×4 | 4320 × 7680 px (9:16 outer) | 1080 × 1920 px each |

These are computed requirements, not assertions that a given generator can produce them. A 2×8 sheet at adequate cell resolution requires approximately 2160×15360 px total — this will likely exceed current generator limits. In that case:
- **Render in batches of 4 pages (2×2 grids)** producing four separate 2×2 collage images
- Or render pages individually in smaller batches (e.g. 4 at a time)
- Disclose actual batch count and delivered asset count in the inventory

Never equate a low-resolution 16-cell thumbnail image with 16 validated standalone pages.

### C. Vertical scroll pipeline

For N pages, a no-gap 1×N vertical scroll has dimensions W × (N×H) and aspect ratio 9:(16N) — not 9:16 unless N=1. Arrange pages in source order. Separators add height; disclose them. Use sectional scrolls and a manifest if one image would be illegible or exceed limits.

### D. Collage-only brief

If explicitly commissioned as a single collage, the collage is the primary output. Its component areas must still be genuinely legible, complete and individually separable for manual slicing. Report: **one composite delivered, N component page areas depicted, zero separate files delivered** (until slices are actually made and checked).

**Manual slicing guidance** (for the commissioner):
- Open the collage in an image editor
- Use the grid coordinates (cell width = total_width ÷ columns; cell height = total_height ÷ rows) to define slice boundaries
- Each 9:16 slice becomes one shareable mobile page
- Verify text legibility and challenge completeness after slicing

### E. Export verification

Before reporting delivery: validate actual canvas dimensions (and per-cell boundaries after slicing), sequence order, readability at target mobile device size, complete final challenge visibility, file existence and actual asset count. If text resolution is insufficient, deliver a clearly named lower-fidelity review preview only; do not call it release-ready.

## 23. Asset identity, lifecycle and dependency invalidation

Use the approved episode archive as logical owner and `CC-ECLPM A8.3 production_manifest` as the primary derivative record family. Supporting record families from CC-ECLPM §4:

`daily_chronicle_record` · `source_snapshot_register` · `source_permission_register` · `challenge_public_register` · `production_manifest` · `character_state_record` · `mission_state_record` · `storyline_register` · `continuity_event_log` · `validation_report` · `exception_conflict_register` · `editorial_decision_log` · `handover_record`

**No new twentieth record family is created by this prompt.** The 19 canonical CC-ECLPM families are sufficient. Use `production_manifest` for comic asset provenance.

For each actual asset, record: episode ID, Output A revision/hash, commission ref, storyboard/page index, page count, prompt/baseline version, character/tech/brand references and versions, consent/license snapshot, challenge text/register revision and reconciliation status, pixel dimensions/format, render attempt ref, QA gate results, editorial/approval evidence, publication evidence and dependency state.

When a source story, public challenge, character design, Edubeam rule, consent status, licensed asset or upstream page changes: perform dependency impact assessment → identify affected pages → mark affected assets `STALE_REVALIDATION_REQUIRED` when actually reviewed/updated → revalidate/regenerate only necessary areas → preserve earlier versions.

## 24. Separate production states; never equate rendering with publication

| Dimension | Truthful states |
|---|---|
| Generation | `NOT_STARTED` / `IN_PROGRESS` / `GENERATED` / `PARTIAL` / `FAILED` |
| QA/technical review | `NOT_CHECKED` / `PASS` / `CHANGES_REQUIRED` / `BLOCKED` |
| Editorial authority | `AWAITING_HUMAN_REVIEW` / `APPROVED` / `REJECTED` / `LOCKED` (evidence required) |
| Assembly/export | `NOT_REQUESTED` / `NOT_STARTED` / `CREATED_UNVERIFIED` / `VALIDATED` / `PARTIAL` / `FAILED` |
| Archive action | `DRAFTED — NOT COMMITTED` / `COMMITTED` (with actual artifact ref) / `INACCESSIBLE` |
| Release/publication | `NOT_AUTHORIZED` / `AUTHORIZED_NOT_PUBLISHED` / `PUBLISHED` (with verifiable destination) / `WITHDRAWN` |
| Dependency currency | `CURRENT` (checked) / `STALE_REVALIDATION_REQUIRED` / `UNVERIFIED` |

Never use a single `COMPLETE` flag. Never conflate a plan, storyboard description or partial render with delivery. A rendered-but-unreviewed image is still a draft.

## 25. Output contract by operating mode

| Mode | Public output | Internal output |
|---|---|---|
| `REVIEW` | Revised documentation | N/A |
| `PLAN` | Beat coverage plan, page allocation, risk report | N/A |
| `STORYBOARD` | Per-page storyboard specs | Internal lettering plan, challenge plan |
| `GENERATE` | Actual 9:16 pages and/or collage + compact delivery status | Per-page production inventory and C01–C22 QA |
| `REVISE` | Modified assets, changed-dependency list | QA evidence, version history |
| `ASSEMBLE` | Actual collage/scroll with geometry verification and inventory | N/A |
| `VALIDATE` | Per-page PASS/FAIL/BLOCKED report | N/A |

When delivering comic art, do not contaminate public images with internal audit tables. Maintain the production record in a separate authorized channel.

---

# PART VI — QA, EXCEPTIONS AND PORTABLE HANDOVER

## 26. Comic-specific visual acceptance gates (C01–C22)

Check **actual rendered pixels**, source documents and permitted records. Use `PASS`, `FAIL`, `BLOCKED`, `NOT_APPLICABLE` or `NOT_CHECKED` with actual observations. A design instruction alone never earns `PASS`.

| Gate | What must be true to pass |
|---|---|
| `C01_COMMISSION` | Exactly authorized mode, source unit, intended outputs and explicit N respected; no extra work claimed |
| `C02_SOURCE_REVISION` | Full eligible Output A and accurate story revision/status known; limited sources properly classified |
| `C03_FIDELITY` | All essential source beats and causal changes conveyed; no consequential fabricated event or omission |
| `C04_PAGE_PURPOSE` | Every page meaningfully advances, deepens or transitions the story without filler |
| `C05_THREE_CLOCKS` | A/B/C clock and physical/imagined modality distinctions correct; no fake date, phase or telemetry |
| `C06_CANON_CHARACTERS` | Identities, ages/eras, abilities, outfits, real-person permissions and visual reference classifications respected |
| `C07_MISSION_TECH` | Edubeam era limits, team deployment/return, technology/equipment state and relationships continuous |
| `C08_ENVIRONMENT` | Sets, lighting, era, period/culture, costumes, weather, props, geography and movement coherent |
| `C09_RIGHTS_CONSENT` | Adaptation, visual references, identifiable likenesses, brands, quotations and release permissions checked |
| `C10_ORIGINALITY` | Original visual expression; no protected-character or third-party distinctive-style imitation |
| `C11_PAGE_ASPECT` | Each individual page verified at 9:16 (1080×1920 px or equivalent), complete, unwarped and uncut |
| `C12_PANEL_FLOW` | Logical eye path, gutters, camera blocking, readable emotion and meaningful visual pacing |
| `C13_LETTERING` | Actual displayed words/spelling/speakers/numbers/labels legible and faithful at mobile size |
| `C14_VISIBLE_LABELS` | No scene/panel numbers; page marks/branding only if permitted; no invented official titles |
| `C15_CHALLENGE_SOURCE` | Exact Output A challenge compared with accessible public register; conflicts resolved or accurately provisional |
| `C16_CHALLENGE_PLACEMENT` | Exact public text once, at bottom of last page, literally nothing visible below it |
| `C17_NO_LEAKAGE` | No visual/textual/metadata answer disclosure; uncertain risk escalated without accessing Output C |
| `C18_COUNT_INVENTORY` | Requested pages vs actual pages/sheets/slices counted and correctly represented; missing pages disclosed |
| `C19_EXPORT_QUALITY` | Actual file/collage geometry, cell ordering, crop, dimensions, sliceability and mobile readability verified |
| `C20_DEPENDENCY` | Source, visual canon, challenge, rights and affected adjacent-page revisions checked for staleness |
| `C21_RECORD_INTEGRITY` | CC-ECLPM-compatible manifest, source/permissions/QA status and truthful commit/approval/publication evidence |
| `C22_DISCLOSURE` | Public, internal and restricted data separated; no unauthorized data exposed to renderers or recipients |

**Cross-check to daily gates:** These comic-specific gates supplement — not rename — the `Q01_SCOPE` through `Q16_BOUNDARIES` gates in CC-DCGP v0.08.00. A comic QA pass does not retroactively certify that the daily chronicle passed unperformed daily gates. Clearly namespace C-gates vs Q-gates in all validation records.

## 27. Exceptions, blocking and safe continuation

| Issue | Correct conduct |
|---|---|
| Source inaccessible or revision ambiguous | Retrieve/reconcile when possible; plan only or limited excerpt draft; no full-source claim |
| v0.08.00 baseline not ratified | Use as requested working candidate; do not claim it supersedes actual approved governance |
| Page count high relative to source | Beat map, FIDELITY_RISK report, fine-grained original-source development; no fabricated events |
| Canon asset missing | Permitted provisional draft only; mark provisional; block approval |
| Locked era / capability conflict | Withhold disputed depiction; register conflicting versions; request editorial decision |
| Real-person consent missing or revoked | Exclude affected material; block release; identify exact requirement |
| Temporal datum unverified | Omit unsupported precision or clearly frame fiction |
| Public challenge missing or differs | Do not invent/rewrite/reveal; provisional or blocked per §16; route to editor |
| Potential challenge leakage | Mark risk; revise; human review if uncertain; never retrieve answer key |
| Renderer garbles text/geometry | Reletter using capable tool; `CHANGES_REQUIRED` if not corrected |
| Composite too small for usable slices | Render individual pages or smaller batches; report preview only; no full-resolution claim |
| Renderer produces partial output | Inventory actual assets; disclose shortfall; no unperformed delivery claims |
| Source/challenge/rights revised mid-run | Recheck pinned sources; mark affected files stale when record actually updated; revalidate |
| Archive/publication tool not accessible | Portable draft manifest locally; `DRAFTED — NOT COMMITTED`; no fabricated commit links |
| Collage cell too small to slice legibly | Increase generator resolution, reduce N per batch, or deliver individual pages; never claim illegible sheet = N pages |

Blocking report minimum: mode, source, N, conflicting/missing prerequisite, evidence checked, material risk, safe work completed, affected pages, required authorized action, disposition.

## 28. ECLPM-compatible comic production delta and handover

On each session's B packet, report:
- **B7. Derivative/media and challenge status:** pages generated/reviewed, collage state, challenge reconciliation status, dependency currency
- **B8. Validation, exceptions and approval register:** per-page C01–C22 outcomes, open conflicts, editorial decisions required, approval evidence
- **B3/B4/B5:** Canon, mission and continuity state affecting comic pages
- **B1/B2:** Source snapshot and research context
- **B6. Portable production handover:** complete self-contained context for next authorized session/provider

Each B6 handover must include: operating mode and commission ref; requested N and actual delivered count with per-page real state; exact Output A ID/revision/status; public challenge text/reference/reconciliation; visual/canon/mission/clock refs; rights snapshot and exclusions; beat map and page-to-page continuity; asset formats/dimensions/real file refs; per-page C01–C22 outcomes; derivative currency and revision risks; unresolved editorial decisions; archive and release state; next authorized action. **Never include the restricted answer or private diary/business secrets.**

## 29. Production pilot and negative test suite

Before authorized adoption, conduct a controlled pilot with an **actually eligible source and human approval**. These are test definitions, not claims they ran:

1. `REVIEW` mode produces only documentation; no image generated or claimed
2. 4-page commission: exactly 4 individual 9:16 pages, each complete, readable, correctly ordered
3. 16-page commission (default): all 16 pages distinct, readable at mobile scale, beat-mapped to Output A, terminal challenge correct
4. Explicit 8-page override: exact 8 pages, 2×4 collage geometry verified, no default substitution
5. 2×2 grid delivery of 4 pages: outer 9:16 sheet, each cell 1080×1920 confirmed after slicing
6. Source excerpt without complete Output A: no full-adaptation claim; coverage limitations disclosed
7. Draft source without publication approval: `DRAFT — NOT FOR PUBLICATION` labels applied
8. Missing official character visual canon: provisional design labeled, not promoted
9. Foundation Phase S00 locked limitations: no unsupported teleportation/return/upgrade depicted
10. Mission team member at deployment point: no unauthorized return scene
11. Clock A/B/C shifts correctly labeled; no fabricated probe UI
12. Real-person consent withdrawn: affected imagery blocked/replaced
13. Third-party logo without distribution rights: not reproduced, not assumed publishable
14. Final challenge full wording including all answer options: fully legible, once, last, not truncated
15. Public challenge register disagrees with Output A: conflict reported, final page blocked
16. Challenge visually implied by panel composition: `C17` FAIL until corrected without key access
17. Generator garbles dialogue: `C13` FAIL until actual typeset correction verified
18. User excludes all numbering: no visible page/panel/scene counters in any delivered image
19. Collage of 16 pages: actual total pixel dimensions reported; per-cell slice dimensions reported; illegible sheet correctly classified as `CHANGES_REQUIRED` not delivered
20. Partial render (tool produces 12 of 16 pages): inventory reports 12 delivered, 4 missing; no false completion claim

## 30. Outstanding decisions requiring the editor or Studio authority

| Decision | Status | Safe default |
|---|---|---|
| Ratification of CC-03.01 v0.08.00 as governing standard | Awaiting owner decision | Use as working candidate; not a supersession claim |
| Approved Classies Chronicles visual identity assets and art direction brief | Unresolved | Original consistent style; no fabricated official marks |
| Canonical character visual references (individual files) | Partially resolved | `EPISODE_ESTABLISHED` or `PROVISIONAL_DRAFT` classification |
| Personal-portrayal consent for each depicted individual | Per-person, per-use | Exclude unverified likenesses; minimum private-person depiction |
| Episode title editorial sign-off | Awaiting last accepted episode component | Provisional titles not printed as official |
| Challenge custody / answer key secure vault | Unresolved | Never generate, access or approximate answer key |
| Approved episode archive paths and write permissions | Unresolved | `DRAFTED — NOT COMMITTED` default |
| Foundation Phase era-locked capability table | Provisional (original story leads only) | Most restrictive depicted capability |
| Authorized batch rendering approach for 16 pages | To be tested per tool | Batches of 4 (2×2 grids) as safe default; verify per tool |
| Mobile export resolution confirmation per platform | Unresolved | 1080×1920 px per page minimum |

---

# PART VII — PORTABLE EXECUTION INSTRUCTION

## 31. Direct reusable production directive

When an authorized AI session receives this prompt alongside an explicit GENERATE commission:

1. **Confirm** mode is `GENERATE` (not `REVIEW`); confirm Output A source revision, N (default 16), delivery form (individual pages + 2×8 collage where possible), rights snapshot
2. **Read** B6 handover if continuing a prior session; verify accepted episode continuity state
3. **Build** beat map from Output A; assign 16 beats/beat-groups to 16 pages using §11 template
4. **Check** canon/rights: classify each character, location, tech reference per §7; verify clock assignments per §8; confirm Edubeam era constraints per §9
5. **Reconcile** public challenge per §16; confirm terminal placement per §17
6. **Storyboard** each page: panel hierarchy (§14), lettering plan (§15), page-purpose test (§12)
7. **Generate** actual 9:16 pages — begin with batches of 4 (2×2 grids) unless tool supports full individual rendering at 1080×1920 px
8. **Verify** each page: check dimensions, readability, challenge placement, answer leakage (§18)
9. **Run** C01–C22 gates on actual rendered pixels (§26)
10. **Assemble** collage where commissioned; verify cell geometry per §22
11. **Compile** delivery manifest: actual delivered count vs N, per-page states, QA summary, open items
12. **Write** B6 handover for next session

## 32. Completion definition

A comic commission is `COMPLETE` when and only when:
- All N commissioned pages have been **actually rendered** and their dimensions verified at 9:16
- All N pages pass `C03_FIDELITY`, `C11_PAGE_ASPECT`, `C13_LETTERING`, `C16_CHALLENGE_PLACEMENT` and `C17_NO_LEAKAGE`
- The terminal public challenge appears once, on the final page, completely, with nothing below it
- The delivery manifest accurately reports the actual delivered asset count
- B6 handover is written and contains all fields from §28
- All QA gate results (PASS/FAIL/BLOCKED/NOT_CHECKED) are recorded with observations

`COMPLETE` does not mean `APPROVED_FOR_PUBLICATION`. Editorial approval and release are separate authorized actions.

## A2. Key changes from CC-MCGP v0.04.00

| Change area | v0.04.00 | v0.08.00 | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| Default page count | 5 pages | **16 pages** | Mobile-first platform requirement: 9:16 portrait pages for WhatsApp Status/Facebook Stories/Snapchat |
| Page format | 9:16 per page | **9:16 per page** (unchanged) | Retained |
| Collage delivery | Optional single collage | **2×8 grid collage as default secondary output**; individual pages primary | Mobile sharing workflow |
| Page allocation template | Five-function rhythm across 5 pages | **16-page template** with explicit page-by-page function assignments | Scale to 16-page default |
| Collage geometry | Underdefined | **Fully specified per batch size** (2×2, 2×4, 2×8, 4×4) with pixel requirements | Prevents false delivery claims |
| Manual slicing guidance | Not included | **Included** (grid coordinates, slice procedure) | Production workflow support |
| Governing master | CC-01.01 v0.04.00 | **CC-01.01 v0.08.00** (candidate) | Alignment with v0.08 production set |

**CC-03.01 v0.08.00** is a complete proposed rewrite of the Master Comic Generation Prompt, produced by synthesizing the full CC-01 and CC-02 v0.08.00 suite against the CC-03.01/CC-03.02 v0.04.00 baseline. The principal change is adoption of the **16-page mobile-first default** (replacing the prior 5-page default) in alignment with the user-stated Note #2 requirement: 9:16 portrait pages suitable for WhatsApp Status, Facebook Stories and Snapchat, with a single collage image as the default secondary output for manual slicing.

All 22 C-gate IDs (C01–C22), the Q01–Q16 cross-reference, the 19 ECLPM canonical families, B1–B8, the challenge firewall and the truthful production-state model are **preserved unchanged** from v0.04.00, in strict compliance with D28 of CC-01.03.

No platform consultation material was therefore available from that source. The update is based on: CC-01.01 through CC-02.04 v0.08.00, CC-03.01/CC-03.02 v0.04.00, and the embedded traceability analysis within those documents.

# 4. Material conflict register and disposition (v0.08.00 additions)

Codes: **IMPLEMENTED** = built into v0.08.00 prompt; **RETAINED** = preexisting requirement preserved; **ADJUSTED** = useful principle corrected; **REJECTED** = contradicts authoritative source; **UPSTREAM DEPENDENCY** = requires authorized records/permissions; **PILOT REQUIRED** = must be evidenced by actual production; **EDITORIAL DECISION** = human ratification necessary.

The full M01–M40 conflict register from CC-03.02 v0.04.00 is fully retained in the v0.08.00 baseline — those dispositions are not reopened unless a conflicting new source supersedes them. The following table records **additional conflicts and decisions arising from the v0.08.00 upgrade**:

| Ref | Identified conflict, omission or recommendation | Treatment in CC-MCGP v0.08.00 | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| `N01` | Prior 5-page default incompatible with mobile-first 9:16 platform requirement (WhatsApp Status, Facebook Stories, Snapchat) | Default changed to 16 individual 9:16 pages per chronicle | IMPLEMENTED (User Note #2) |
| `N02` | 4 prior 9:16 pages = 1 prior landscape/A4 page: scaling factor documented | §11 page allocation template maps 16 pages across narrative arc; prior 5-page equivalent ≈ 20 pages at same coverage depth | IMPLEMENTED |
| `N03` | Single collage of all pages requested for manual slicing | Default secondary output is a 2×8 grid collage (or batch equivalents); individual pages primary | IMPLEMENTED (User Note #2) |
| `N04` | 2×8 grid at 1080×1920 per cell requires 2160×15360 px total — likely exceeds current generator limits | §22B specifies batches of 4 (2×2 grids) as safe alternative; user slices manually from batch outputs | ADJUSTED — feasibility preserved |
| `N05` | Outer collage aspect ratio for 2×8 grid of 9:16 cells is 9:64, not 9:16 | §22B table fully enumerates outer geometry per grid configuration; C19 verification required | IMPLEMENTED |
| `N06` | Manual slicing guidance was absent from v0.04.00 | §22D adds explicit slicing guidance (grid coordinates, slice procedure, legibility verification) | IMPLEMENTED |
| `N07` | Governing document references in v0.04.00 pointed to CC-01.01/02.01/02.02/04.01 v0.04.00 | §A1 source registry updated to v0.08.00 document family (CC-01.01 through CC-02.04) | IMPLEMENTED |
| `N08` | Responses-3 compilation was empty (0 bytes) | Noted in §A1; no platform consultation material available; update based on available sources only | DOCUMENTED — no synthetic content invented |
| `N09` | 16-page narrative template was not defined at v0.04.00 | §11 defines a 16-page page-function template (hook/orientation/development/event/consequences/reflection/challenge) | IMPLEMENTED |
| `N10` | Extended, complex and exceptional page counts need rescaling for mobile-first standard | §11 updated to 4–6 (short), 20–24 (extended), 24–28 (complex), 32 (exceptional) | ADJUSTED |
| `N11` | C01–C22 gate IDs must not change (D28) | Verified: C01–C22 are identical to v0.04.00 in §26 | RETAINED — D28 compliance |
| `N12` | Q01–Q16 gate IDs must not change | Verified: Q01–Q16 cross-reference in §26 is identical to v0.04.00 | RETAINED |

## 5.3 Mobile geometry decision (carries forward from v0.04.00 §8, updated)

For a 16-page commission:

| Delivery configuration | Pixel geometry | Outer aspect | Per-cell requirement | Feasibility |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 16 individual pages | 16 × (1080 × 1920) | 9:16 each | 1080 × 1920 px | Preferred; requires 16 render calls |
| 2×8 grid collage | 2160 × 15360 px | 9:64 | 1080 × 1920 px | High pixel total; likely exceeds generators |
| 4×4 grid collage | 4320 × 7680 px | 9:16 outer | 1080 × 1920 px | High pixel total; outer ratio coincidentally 9:16 |
| 4 × (2×2 batch) | 4 sheets, each 2160 × 3840 | 9:16 per sheet | 1080 × 1920 px | **Recommended safe default** |
| 4 × (4-page scroll) | 4 × (1080 × 7680) | 9:64 each | 1080 × 1920 px | Alternative; 4 separate scrolls |

**Recommended safe production path for 16-page commission:** render in four batches of 4 pages each (four 2×2 collage images), verify each batch, then compile if required. This keeps total pixel dimensions within 2160×3840 px per image, which most current generators support.

The outer grid ratio is documentation convenience only. Individual page 9:16 compliance (C11) is the binding requirement.

## 5.4 Challenge and release decisions (carried forward and confirmed)

The challenge firewall is unchanged:
- Output C is never accessed, approximated or included in any comic output
- `challenge_public_register` reconciliation is required before a final page is release-eligible
- A provisional draft final page is allowed when the register is inaccessible and governance permits
- Nothing appears below the terminal challenge on the final page
- C17 leakage check uses public question and story text only; uncertain cases escalate to an authorized human reviewer

## 5.5 Rules for resolving conflicts between v0.04.00 and v0.08.00

1. Where v0.08.00 governing documents (CC-01.01/02/03/04) introduce a new requirement or correct an error found in v0.04.00, the v0.08.00 version applies.
2. Where v0.04.00 CC-03.01/02 establishes a constraint not mentioned in v0.08.00 governing documents (e.g. specific C-gate definitions), the v0.04.00 constraint is preserved unless explicitly superseded.
3. Gate IDs (C01–C22, Q01–Q16) are frozen; no renaming at any version transition.
4. The user's Note #2 (16-page mobile default, collage, manual slicing) is treated as an explicit commission-level override that supersedes the prior 5-page default.
5. A higher version number without editorial ratification does not displace an approved lower-version standard. Both v0.04.00 and v0.08.00 remain proposed candidates until the production owner decides.

# 7. Mobile geometry and media feasibility (v0.08.00 update)

Carries forward the exact table from CC-03.02 v0.04.00 §8, extended for 16-page commissions:

| N pages | Delivery configuration | Outer geometry | Per-cell | Feasibility note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Single page | 1080 × 1920 | 1080 × 1920 | Standard; always feasible |
| 4 | 2×2 grid | 2160 × 3840 | 1080 × 1920 | **Safe default for a 4-page batch** |
| 8 | 2×4 grid | 2160 × 7680 | 1080 × 1920 | May exceed generator; use two 2×2 batches |
| 16 | 4×4 grid | 4320 × 7680 | 1080 × 1920 | High resolution; test generator capability |
| 16 | 2×8 grid | 2160 × 15360 | 1080 × 1920 | Very high; likely exceeds most generators |
| 16 | 4 × (2×2) batches | 4 × 2160 × 3840 | 1080 × 1920 | **Recommended safe path** |
| Any N | N individual files | N × 1080 × 1920 | 1080 × 1920 | Preferred for quality; N render calls |

**Slicing formula** (for any M-column × R-row grid):
- Cell width = total_width ÷ M
- Cell height = total_height ÷ R
- Cell at position (col, row) starts at pixel (col × cell_width, row × cell_height)
- Slice page index = row × M + col + 1 (0-indexed col/row)

---

Unchanged from CC-03.02 v0.04.00 §9. Confirmed compatible with CC-01.01 v0.08.00 §27 and CC-02.01 v0.08.00 Q15/Q16 challenge gates. No new authority or policy changes are introduced.

The mobile-first format has no effect on challenge governance: the terminal challenge occupies the bottom of page 16 (or page N), nothing appears below it, the answer key is never accessed, and the public register reconciliation requirement is unchanged.

# 10. Approval, roll-out and rollback

## 10.1 Adoption prerequisites

1. Owner confirms which approved franchise/Studio and canon versions govern the pilot
2. Owner/editor approves CC-03.01 v0.08.00 and CC-03.02 v0.08.00 without overwriting v0.04.00 historical record
3. Select an actually accessible, fully identified Output A revision and determine its status
4. Verify permitted visual canon, brand license, personal-portrayal consent, Edubeam era constraints, public challenge source
5. Confirm batch rendering approach and per-cell resolution capability
6. Conduct a 4-page pilot (one 2×2 batch), then a 16-page full commission test; measure actual results
7. Record all tested pages/files, source/rights/canon/checks and editorial decisions in ECLPM-compatible records
8. Only after QA and an actual separate human release decision may publication proceed

## 10.2 Failure/rollback

A failure in canonical source reconciliation, visual consent, answer-leak safeguards, essential narrative fidelity or actual renderer capacity is a release blocker. Preserve prior accepted assets; flag new draft and affected dependencies. If v0.08.00 must be reversed, re-enable the last actually approved version, preserve both draft and accepted revision history, inspect derivative dependencies.

## 10.3 Acceptance evidence matrix

| Test | Minimum evidence | Typical failure to detect |
|---|---|---|
| 16-page default applied | Storyboard with 16 page-purpose entries; 16 delivered assets | 5 pages delivered when 16 commissioned |
| 2×2 batch geometry | Actual cell dimensions after render; counted slice files | 16-cell thumbnail misrepresented as 16 full pages |
| Individual 9:16 compliance | Per-page pixel dimensions; C11 PASS | Outer collage 9:16 substituted for individual page check |
| Manual slicing feasibility | Actual sliced files at 1080×1920 each; text legibility confirmed | Cells too small to yield readable slices |
| Beat coverage at 16 pages | Beat-to-page coverage table; no invented events | Fabricated plot filler on pages 8–12 |
| Terminal challenge | Final page pixel inspection; nothing below challenge | Challenge truncated or followed by page count |
| Mode isolation | REVIEW produces only documentation | Images generated from a document-update request |
| Partial render inventory | Delivered count accurately reported | 12 pages delivered, 16 claimed |
